typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a><span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>(light green)<span facto</a></i> impossible since 2018, explicitly illegal since 2020.<sup about="#mwt5" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-transvanilla_1-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"transvanilla"}}'><a href="#cite_note-transvanilla-1" id="mwCQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwCg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwCw">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwDA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt7" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Transgender_People_In_Hungary_Lose_Right_To_Gender_Recognition_2-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Transgender People In Hungary Lose Right To Gender Recognition"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Transgender_People_In_Hungary_Lose_Right_To_Gender_Recognition-2" id="mwDQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwDg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwDw">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwEA">]</span></span></a></sup></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_people_and_military_service" title="LGBT people and military service" class="mw-redirect">Military</a></th><td class="infobox-data">LGB people allowed to openly serve, transgender people not allowed.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-data">Sexual orientation and gender identity protections (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="#Discrimination_protections" class="mw-selflink-fragment">see below</a>)</td></tr><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-header">Family rights</th></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template:Same-sex_unions" title="Template:Same-sex unions">Recognition of relationships</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Hungary" title="Recognition of same-sex unions in Hungary">Unregistered cohabitation since 1996,<br/>Registered partnerships since 2009</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Restrictions</th><td class="infobox-data">Same-sex marriage constitutionally banned</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_adoption" title="Same-sex adoption">Adoption</a></th><td class="infobox-data">Same-sex couples adopting children has been constitutionally banned since 2020.<sup about="#mwt9" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-adoption2020_3-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"adoption2020"}}'><a href="#cite_note-adoption2020-3" id="mwEQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwEg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwEw">[</span>3<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwFA">]</span></span></a></sup></td></tr></tbody></table>

<p id="mwFQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lesbian" title="Lesbian" id="mwFg">Lesbian</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gay" title="Gay" id="mwFw">gay</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bisexuality" title="Bisexuality" id="mwGA">bisexual</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transgender" title="Transgender" id="mwGQ">transgender</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queer" title="Queer" id="mwGg">queer</a> (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_people" title="LGBTQ people" id="mwGw">LGBTQ</a>) people in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungary" title="Hungary" id="mwHA">Hungary</a> face significant challenges not experienced by non-LGBTQ residents, although the view of LGBTQ people and LGBTQ rights in Hungarian society has slightly improved in recent years. <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homosexuality" title="Homosexuality" id="mwHQ">Homosexuality</a> is legal in Hungary for both men and women. Discrimination on the basis of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_orientation" title="Sexual orientation" id="mwHg">sexual orientation</a> and sex is banned in the country. Households headed by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_couples" title="Same-sex couples" class="mw-redirect" id="mwHw">same-sex couples</a> are not eligible for all of the same legal rights available to heterosexual married couples. <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Hungary" title="Recognition of same-sex unions in Hungary" id="mwIA">Registered partnership</a> for same-sex couples was legalised in 2009, but <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage" title="Same-sex marriage" id="mwIQ">same-sex marriage</a> remains banned.</p>

<p id="mwIg">Under the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_premiership_of_Viktor_Orbán" title="Second premiership of Viktor Orbán" id="mwIw">far-right government</a> of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viktor_Orbán" title="Viktor Orbán" id="mwJA">Viktor Orbán</a>, the Hungarian government began to enact a number of anti-LGBTQ policies in the 2020s, including a 2020 bill which ended legal recognition of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity" title="Gender identity" id="mwJQ">gender identity</a>, a resolution against the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istanbul_Convention" title="Istanbul Convention" id="mwJg">Istanbul Convention</a> due to its alleged promotion of "gender ideology", a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarian_anti-LGBTQ_law" title="Hungarian anti-LGBTQ law" id="mwJw">2021 "child protection" law</a> prohibiting the distribution of content promoting homosexuality or <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_transition" title="Gender transition" id="mwKA">gender transitions</a> to minors, a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarian_Pride_parade_ban" title="Hungarian Pride parade ban" id="mwKQ">2025 law</a> banning public assemblies that violate the child protection law (such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pride_parade" title="Pride parade" id="mwKg">parades</a>), and an amendment to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fundamental_Law_of_Hungary" title="Fundamental Law of Hungary" id="mwKw">Fundamental Law</a> codifying gender as a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_binary" title="Gender binary" id="mwLA">male-female binary</a>. After being denied police permits due to the pride parade ban, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budapest_Pride" title="Budapest Pride" id="mwLQ">Budapest Pride</a> went on as a demonstration against the Orbán government, and Mayor <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gergely_Karácsony" title="Gergely Karácsony" id="mwLg">Gergely Karácsony</a> was charged with holding an illegal public assembly.</p>

<p id="mwLw">In April 2026, Orbán was defeated in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Hungarian_parliamentary_election" title="2026 Hungarian parliamentary election" id="mwMA">the 2026 parliamentary election</a> by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Péter_Magyar" title="Péter Magyar" id="mwMQ">Péter Magyar</a>'s <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tisza_Party" title="Tisza Party" id="mwMg">Tisza Party</a>. Shortly afterward, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Court_of_Justice" title="European Court of Justice" id="mwMw">European Court of Justice</a> (ECJ) ruled that the Orbán-era "child protection" laws violated the EU <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charter_of_Fundamental_Rights_of_the_European_Union" title="Charter of Fundamental Rights of the European Union" id="mwNA">Charter of Fundamental Rights</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_the_European_Union" title="Treaty of the European Union" class="mw-redirect" id="mwNQ">Treaty of the European Union</a>, as it infringed upon <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_speech" title="Freedom of speech" id="mwNg">freedom of expression</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fundamental_rights" title="Fundamental rights" id="mwNw">fundamental rights</a>. The Budapest police reversed its ban on Budapest Pride and dropped its charges against Mayor Karácsony, while Magyar announced the government's intent to amend the child protection law to remove its LGBTQ-related provisions. </p>

<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" id="mwOA" data-mw='{"autoGenerated":true}'/></section><section data-mw-section-id="1" id="mwOQ" aria-labelledby="Law_regarding_same-sex_sexual_activity"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Law_regarding_same-sex_sexual_activity">Law regarding same-sex sexual activity</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Hungary&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Law regarding same-sex sexual activity"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwOg">The first <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary" title="Kingdom of Hungary" id="mwOw">Hungarian</a> Penal Code by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Károly_Csemegi" title="Károly Csemegi" id="mwPA">Károly Csemegi</a> (1878) punished homosexuality between men ("természet elleni fajtalanság" – <i id="mwPQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crime_against_nature" title="Crime against nature" id="mwPg">perversion against nature</a> (nature's law)</i>) with prison up to one year. Homosexual activity above the age of 20 was decriminalized in 1961, then above the age of 18 in 1978 by the new Penal Code. The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_consent" title="Age of consent" id="mwPw">age of consent</a>, which is 14, has applied equally to heterosexual and homosexual activity since a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Hungary" title="Constitutional Court of Hungary" id="mwQA">Constitutional Court</a> decision of 2002. Lesbian, gay and bisexual people are not banned from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_orientation_and_military_service_by_country" title="Sexual orientation and military service by country" id="mwQQ">openly serving in the military service</a>.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="2" id="mwQg" aria-labelledby="Recognition_of_same-sex_relationships"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Recognition_of_same-sex_relationships">Recognition of same-sex relationships</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Hungary&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Recognition of same-sex relationships"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt11" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwQw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Main","href":"./Template:Main"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Recognition of same-sex unions in Hungary"}},"i":0}}]}'><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1368532237" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" about="#mwt12" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'>.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+.mw-empty-elt+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}@media print{body.ns-0 .mw-parser-output .hatnote{display:none!important}}</style></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt11" id="mwRA">Main article: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recognition_of_same-sex_unions_in_Hungary" title="Recognition of same-sex unions in Hungary">Recognition of same-sex unions in Hungary</a></div>
<p id="mwRQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unregistered_cohabitation" title="Unregistered cohabitation" id="mwRg">Unregistered cohabitation</a> has been recognised since 1996. It applies to any couple living together in an economic and sexual relationship (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common-law_marriage" title="Common-law marriage" id="mwRw">common-law marriage</a>), including same-sex couples. No official registration is required. The law gives some specified rights and benefits to two persons living together. Unregistered cohabitation is defined in the Civil Code as "when two persons are living together outside of wedlock in an emotional and financial community in the same household, provided that neither of them is engaged in wedlock or partnership with another person, registered or otherwise, and that they are not related in direct line, and they are not siblings." <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inheritance" title="Inheritance" id="mwSA">Inheritance</a> is possible only with <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Will_and_testament" title="Will and testament" id="mwSQ">testament</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Widow's_pension" title="Widow's pension" id="mwSg">widow-pension</a> is available for couples cohabiting for more than 10 years.</p>

<p id="mwSw">On 17 December 2007, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_Hungary" title="Parliament of Hungary" class="mw-redirect" id="mwTA">Parliament</a> adopted a registered partnership bill submitted by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarian_Socialist_Party" title="Hungarian Socialist Party" id="mwTQ">Hungarian Socialist Party</a>–<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance_of_Free_Democrats" title="Alliance of Free Democrats" id="mwTg">Alliance of Free Democrats</a> Government. The bill was found <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutionality" title="Constitutionality" id="mwTw">unconstitutional</a> by the Constitutional Court because it duplicated the institution of marriage for opposite-sex couples. In February 2009, the Parliament approval a modified version of the bill. Since 1 July 2009, same-sex couples can enter into <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Registered_partnerships" title="Registered partnerships" class="mw-redirect" id="mwUA">registered partnerships</a>. The law gives the same rights to registered partners as to spouses except for <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adoption" title="Adoption" id="mwUQ">adoption</a>, assisted reproduction or taking a surname.<sup about="#mwt15" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-pink_4-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"pink"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-pink-4"}}'><a href="#cite_note-pink-4" id="mwUg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwUw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwVA">[</span>4<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwVQ">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt18" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Reuters_5-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Reuters"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Reuters-5"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Reuters-5" id="mwVg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwVw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwWA">[</span>5<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwWQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwWg">On 1 January 2012, a new <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Hungary#2011_Constitution" title="Constitution of Hungary" class="mw-redirect" id="mwWw">constitution</a>, enacted by the Parliament in 2011, came into effect, restricting marriage to opposite-sex couples and containing no guarantees of protection from discrimination on account of sexual orientation. Viktor Orbán, leader of the ruling Fidesz party, which recently came to power with a two-thirds majority in the parliament, has been dubbed “Viktator” by the crowds protesting the new constitution.<sup about="#mwt22" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-6" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-6"}}'><a href="#cite_note-6" id="mwXA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwXQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwXg">[</span>6<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwXw">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt25" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-7" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-7"}}'><a href="#cite_note-7" id="mwYA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwYQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwYg">[</span>7<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwYw">]</span></span></a></sup> However, discrimination on the basis of sexual orientation remains banned through interpretation of the general non-discrimination provision in the Constitution, as well as by the <i id="mwZA">Equal Treatment Act</i>.<link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_articles_with_unsourced_statements" about="#mwt19" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwZQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Citation needed","href":"./Template:Citation_needed"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"December 2020"}},"i":0}}]}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_unsourced_statements_from_December_2020" about="#mwt19"/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt19" id="mwZg"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="3" id="mwZw" aria-labelledby="Adoption_and_family_planning"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Adoption_and_family_planning">Adoption and family planning</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Hungary&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Adoption and family planning"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwaA">Although same-sex couples cannot adopt jointly, adoption by individuals is illegal regardless of sexual orientation or partnership status. Stepchild adoption is only available for married (different-sex) couples.</p>

<p id="mwaQ">Access to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IVF" title="IVF" class="mw-redirect" id="mwag">IVF</a> and donor insemination is available for single women regardless of sexual orientation, but not available for lesbians cohabiting or in a registered partnership with their same-sex partners.</p>

<p id="mwaw">In November 2017, the Hungarian <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ombudsman" title="Ombudsman" id="mwbA">Ombudsman</a> found that the rejection of a lesbian couple's adoption application was "an infringement on the child's right to protection and care, and amounted to unlawful discrimination based on sexual orientation." As joint <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_adoption" title="Same-sex adoption" id="mwbQ">adoption for same-sex couples</a> is not legal in Hungary, the couple decided that one of the partners would legally adopt the child. The couple was, however, very open about their relationship and were found suitable to adopt. During the following months, the couple took care of a 16-month-old girl, but child protection services later stopped the application procedure due to the couple's sexual orientation. This decision disrupted the life of the child, as she would not eat properly anymore and had to be taken to a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_psychologist" title="Child psychologist" class="mw-redirect" id="mwbg">child psychologist</a>. The couple appealed to the Commissioner for Fundamental Rights (the Ombudsman responsible for the rights of children, nationalities in Hungary, vulnerable social groups and the interests of future generations), who found the child protection service's rejection of the couple unlawful and discriminatory. The Commissioner said that "a person wishing to adopt has no right to adopt a particular child, but s/he does have the right to equal treatment and equality before the law in the procedure." The Commissioner based their decision on the 2008 <i id="mwbw">E.B. v. France</i> case, in which the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Court_of_Human_Rights" title="European Court of Human Rights" id="mwcA">European Court of Human Rights</a> ruled that one's sexual orientation should not be a factor in adoption cases.<sup about="#mwt28" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-8" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-8"}}'><a href="#cite_note-8" id="mwcQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwcg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwcw">[</span>8<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwdA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwdQ">In October 2020, while discussing a children's book published by an LGBTQ organisation on <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magyar_Rádió" title="Magyar Rádió" id="mwdg">Magyar Rádió</a>, Prime Minister Orbán stated that, despite Hungary being "tolerant and patient" towards LGBTQ people, "there is a red line that cannot be crossed" and that "gays are to leave our children alone".<sup about="#mwt31" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-9" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-9"}}'><a href="#cite_note-9" id="mwdw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mweA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mweQ">[</span>9<span class="cite-bracket" id="mweg">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt34" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-10" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-10"}}'><a href="#cite_note-10" id="mwew"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwfA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwfQ">[</span>10<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwfg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwfw">In November 2020, the Fidesz government proposed a constitutional amendment which would ban <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adoption_by_same-sex_couples" title="Adoption by same-sex couples" class="mw-redirect" id="mwgA">adoption by same-sex couples</a>. Language in the amendment would ensure "education in accordance with the values based on Hungary's constitutional identity and Christian culture." The same amendment would also severely restrict the ability of single-parent families to adopt.<sup about="#mwt37" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-11" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-11"}}'><a href="#cite_note-11" id="mwgQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwgg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwgw">[</span>11<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwhA">]</span></span></a></sup> On 16 December 2020 the amendment was passed by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_(Hungary)" title="National Assembly (Hungary)" id="mwhQ">National Assembly</a> with 123 ayes, 45 nays and 5 abstentions.<sup about="#mwt40" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-12" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-12"}}'><a href="#cite_note-12" id="mwhg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwhw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwiA">[</span>12<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwiQ">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt43" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-13" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-13"}}'><a href="#cite_note-13" id="mwig"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwiw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwjA">[</span>13<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwjQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwjg">In April 2023, Hungary's unicameral parliament passed a bill enabling and encouraging citizens to report same-sex couples which raise children to the state authorities - such as police and child protection.<sup about="#mwt46" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-14" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-14"}}'><a href="#cite_note-14" id="mwjw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwkA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwkQ">[</span>14<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwkg">]</span></span></a></sup> The President of Hungary <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katalin_Novák" title="Katalin Novák" id="mwkw">Katalin Novák</a> vetoed the bill - that formally prevented it from becoming enacted into law.<sup about="#mwt49" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-15" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-15"}}'><a href="#cite_note-15" id="mwlA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwlQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwlg">[</span>15<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwlw">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt52" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-16" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-16"}}'><a href="#cite_note-16" id="mwmA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwmQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwmg">[</span>16<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwmw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="4" id="mwnA" aria-labelledby="Discrimination_protections"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Discrimination_protections">Discrimination protections</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Hungary&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Discrimination protections"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwnQ">In 2000, the Constitutional Court recognized that the constitutional ban on discrimination based on "other status" covers <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_orientation" title="Sexual orientation" id="mwng">sexual orientation</a> as well. The <i id="mwnw">Act on Public Health</i> has banned sexual orientation-related discrimination in health services since 1997 and gender identity-related discrimination since 2004.<sup about="#mwt54" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-rainbow_17-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"rainbow"}}'><a href="#cite_note-rainbow-17" id="mwoA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwoQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwog">[</span>17<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwow">]</span></span></a></sup> The <i id="mwpA">2003 Act on Equal Treatment and the Promotion of Equal Opportunities</i> (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarian_language" title="Hungarian language" about="#mwt53" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwpQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"langx","href":"./Template:Langx"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"hu"},"2":{"wt":"2003. évi CXXV. törvény az egyenlő bánásmódról és az esélyegyenlőség előmozdításáról"}},"i":0}}]}'>Hungarian</a><span about="#mwt53">: </span><i lang="hu" about="#mwt53">2003. évi CXXV. törvény az egyenlő bánásmódról és az esélyegyenlőség előmozdításáról</i><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_Hungarian-language_text" about="#mwt53" id="mwpg"/>), which took effect in January 2004, forbids discrimination based on factors that include sexual orientation and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity" title="Gender identity" id="mwpw">gender identity</a> in the fields of employment, education, housing, health, and access to goods and services.<sup about="#mwt55" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-rainbow_17-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"rainbow"}}'><a href="#cite_note-rainbow-17" id="mwqA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwqQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwqg">[</span>17<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwqw">]</span></span></a></sup> Article 8 of the Act states as follows:</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1244412712" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt56" id="mwrA" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Template:Blockquote/styles.css"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"cquote","href":"./Template:Cquote"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Provisions that result in a person or a group [being] treated less favourably than another person or group in a comparable situation because of his/her sex, racial origin, colour, nationality, national or ethnic origin, mother tongue, disability, state of health, religious or ideological conviction, political or other opinion, family status, motherhood (pregnancy) or fatherhood, sexual orientation, gender identity, age, social origin, financial status, the part-time nature or definite term of the employment relationship or other relationship related to employment, the membership of an organisation representing employees&apos; interests, [and any] other status, attribute or characteristic are considered direct discrimination."}},"i":0}}]}'>.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 32px}.mw-parser-output .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;margin-top:0}@media(min-width:500px){.mw-parser-output .templatequotecite{padding-left:1.6em}}</style><blockquote class="templatequote " about="#mwt56" id="mwrQ"><p>Provisions that result in a person or a group [being] treated less favourably than another person or group in a comparable situation because of his/her sex, racial origin, colour, nationality, national or ethnic origin, mother tongue, disability, state of health, religious or ideological conviction, political or other opinion, family status, motherhood (pregnancy) or fatherhood, sexual orientation, gender identity, age, social origin, financial status, the part-time nature or definite term of the employment relationship or other relationship related to employment, the membership of an organisation representing employees' interests, [and any] other status, attribute or characteristic are considered direct discrimination.</p></blockquote>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="5" id="mwrg" aria-labelledby="Gender_identity_and_expression"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Gender_identity_and_expression">Gender identity and expression</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Hungary&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Gender identity and expression"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwrw">In December 2017, a government decree was published, establishing for the first time a legal basis for gender transitions. After 1 January 2018, transgender people living in Hungary were theoretically able to change their legal gender. They required a diagnosis from a medical professional, but did not have to undergo hormone therapy, sterilization or <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender-affirming_surgery" title="Gender-affirming surgery" id="mwsA">gender-affirming surgery</a>.<sup about="#mwt60" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-18" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-18"}}'><a href="#cite_note-18" id="mwsQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwsg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwsw">[</span>18<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwtA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt63" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-rainbow_17-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"rainbow"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-rainbow-17"}}'><a href="#cite_note-rainbow-17" id="mwtQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwtg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwtw">[</span>17<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwuA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt66" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-19" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-19"}}'><a href="#cite_note-19" id="mwuQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwug"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwuw">[</span>19<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwvA">]</span></span></a></sup> However, Transvanilla – an organization based in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budapest" title="Budapest" id="mwvQ">Budapest</a>, which campaigns on behalf of transgender rights – reports that the government has refused to honor applications of the legal gender change since 2018. In 2019, a joint case of 23 people was created and submitted to the European Court of Human Rights.<sup about="#mwt69" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-transvanilla_1-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"transvanilla"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-transvanilla-1"}}'><a href="#cite_note-transvanilla-1" id="mwvg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwvw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwwA">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwwQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwwg">In 2018, the Hungarian government removed accreditation from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_studies" title="Gender studies" id="mwww">gender studies</a> programs at Hungarian universities, arguing that there was no market for gender studies graduates. Bence Rétvári, the political undersecretary in the ministry of Human Resources justified the decision by claiming that, "Gender Studies - similarly to Marxism-Leninism - can be called an ideology rather than a science."<sup about="#mwt72" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-20" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-20"}}'><a href="#cite_note-20" id="mwxA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwxQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwxg">[</span>20<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwxw">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt75" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-21" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-21"}}'><a href="#cite_note-21" id="mwyA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwyQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwyg">[</span>21<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwyw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwzA">While Hungary, alongside the majority of EU countries, has signed the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istanbul_Convention" title="Istanbul Convention" id="mwzQ">Istanbul Convention</a> treaty that aims to combat and prevent violence against women, it has refused to take further action to ratify the Convention. In May 2020, the National Assembly adopted a resolution that rejected the Istanbul Convention, and supports the government declaration that claims that the measures of the treaty promote “destructive gender ideologies” and “illegal migration.”<sup about="#mwt78" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-22" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-22"}}'><a href="#cite_note-22" id="mwzg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwzw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw0A">[</span>22<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw0Q">]</span></span></a></sup> The declaration was adopted with 115 votes in favor, 35 against and three abstentions. The Hungarian government, including the Ministry of Justice, argued that the Hungarian law already contains a “comprehensive system for assisting and protecting victims.” <sup about="#mwt81" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-23" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-23"}}'><a href="#cite_note-23" id="mw0g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw0w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw1A">[</span>23<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw1Q">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mw1g">On 19 May 2020, the National Assembly voted 134–56 (with 4 abstaining) in favour of a bill that ended legal recognition of transgender and intersex persons; the bill enshrines a definition of sex (<i id="mw1w">nem</i>, which was previously interpreted as referring to both sex and gender) in federal law as an immutable male-female binary determined at birth which cannot be changed (<i id="mw2A">születési nem</i>).<sup about="#mwt84" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-anti-trans-bill_24-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"anti-trans-bill"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-anti-trans-bill-24"}}'><a href="#cite_note-anti-trans-bill-24" id="mw2Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw2g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw2w">[</span>24<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw3A">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt87" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-25" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-25"}}'><a href="#cite_note-25" id="mw3Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw3g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw3w">[</span>25<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw4A">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt90" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-anti-trans-bill-article_26-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"anti-trans-bill-article"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-anti-trans-bill-article-26"}}'><a href="#cite_note-anti-trans-bill-article-26" id="mw4Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw4g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw4w">[</span>26<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw5A">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt91" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Transgender_People_In_Hungary_Lose_Right_To_Gender_Recognition_2-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Transgender People In Hungary Lose Right To Gender Recognition"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Transgender_People_In_Hungary_Lose_Right_To_Gender_Recognition-2" id="mw5Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw5g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw5w">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw6A">]</span></span></a></sup> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dunja_Mijatović" title="Dunja Mijatović" id="mw6Q">Dunja Mijatović</a>, commissioner for human rights in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe" title="Council of Europe" id="mw6g">Council of Europe</a>, stated it "contravenes human rights standards and the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_LGBT-related_cases_before_international_courts_and_quasi-judicial_bodies#European_Court_of_Human_Rights" title="List of LGBT-related cases before international courts and quasi-judicial bodies" class="mw-redirect" id="mw6w">case law of the European Court of Human Rights</a>".<sup about="#mwt94" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-BuzzFeed_27-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"BuzzFeed"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-BuzzFeed-27"}}'><a href="#cite_note-BuzzFeed-27" id="mw7A"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw7Q"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw7g">[</span>27<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw7w">]</span></span></a></sup> President János Áder signed the bill into law on 28 May 2020.<sup about="#mwt97" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-tgeu-20200529_28-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"tgeu-20200529"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-tgeu-20200529-28"}}'><a href="#cite_note-tgeu-20200529-28" id="mw8A"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw8Q"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw8g">[</span>28<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw8w">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mw9A">In January 2021 the government ordered that a book published by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labrisz_Lesbian_Association" title="Labrisz Lesbian Association" id="mw9Q">Labrisz Lesbian Association</a> carry warnings saying it "[contains] behaviour inconsistent with traditional gender roles".<sup about="#mwt100" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Guardian_2021-01-20_29-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Guardian 2021-01-20"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Guardian_2021-01-20-29"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Guardian_2021-01-20-29" id="mw9g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw9w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw-A">[</span>29<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw-Q">]</span></span></a></sup> According to a government spokesperson, "the book is sold as a fairytale... but it hides the fact that it depicts behaviour inconsistent with traditional gender roles."<sup about="#mwt101" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Guardian_2021-01-20_29-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Guardian 2021-01-20"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Guardian_2021-01-20-29" id="mw-g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw-w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw_A">[</span>29<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw_Q">]</span></span></a></sup> In response, the association announced that they would be filing suit.<sup about="#mwt102" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Guardian_2021-01-20_29-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Guardian 2021-01-20"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Guardian_2021-01-20-29" id="mw_g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw_w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQA">[</span>29<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQE">]</span></span></a></sup>  After the publication of thebook, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fidesz" title="Fidesz" id="mwAQI">Fidesz</a> also launched a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smear_campaign" title="Smear campaign" id="mwAQM">smear campaign</a> against the organization and the entire LGBTQ community, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_grooming_conspiracy_theory" title="LGBTQ grooming conspiracy theory" id="mwAQQ">deliberately confusing homosexuality with pedophilia</a>. Labrisz took the government-related media giant Mediaworks to court too, for an article that called them pedophiles without any proof. In the lawsuits, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights" title="Human rights" id="mwAQU">human rights</a> NGO <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarian_Helsinki_Committee" title="Hungarian Helsinki Committee" id="mwAQY">Hungarian Helsinki Committee</a> represented Labrisz, and they won at first instance. However, in November 2022, the Curia (the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curia_of_Hungary" title="Curia of Hungary" id="mwAQc">Curia</a> found that the article did not violate Labrisz's right to good reputation. The human rights defenders don't accept this decision - claiming double standards and that the government uses the freedom expression as an excuse to cover hatred and abuse - and turn to the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Hungary" title="Constitutional Court of Hungary" id="mwAQg">Constitutional Court</a>.<sup about="#mwt105" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-30" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-30"}}'><a href="#cite_note-30" id="mwAQk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAQo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQs">[</span>30<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAQ0">In April 2023, Hungary implemented a law enabling citizens to report to the state people who "contest" a child's right to "an identity appropriate to their sex at birth".<sup about="#mwt108" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-31" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-31"}}'><a href="#cite_note-31" id="mwAQ4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAQ8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARA">[</span>31<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARE">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwARI">In April 2025, a constitutional amendment was passed to enshrine in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fundamental_Law_of_Hungary" title="Fundamental Law of Hungary" id="mwARM">Fundamental Law</a> that there are only two genders, male and female, and that rights to a child's "proper physical, mental and moral development" takes precedence over all other fundamental rights aside from the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_to_life" title="Right to life" id="mwARQ">right to life</a>.<sup about="#mwt111" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-32" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-32"}}'><a href="#cite_note-32" id="mwARU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwARY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARc">[</span>32<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARg">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt114" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-33" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-33"}}'><a href="#cite_note-33" id="mwARk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwARo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARs">[</span>33<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARw">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt117" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-34" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-34"}}'><a href="#cite_note-34" id="mwAR0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAR4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAR8">[</span>34<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt120" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-35" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-35"}}'><a href="#cite_note-35" id="mwASE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwASI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASM">[</span>35<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="6" id="mwASU" aria-labelledby="Blood_donation"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Blood_donation">Blood donation</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Hungary&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Blood donation"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt121" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwASY" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"See also","href":"./Template:See_also"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Blood donation restrictions on men who have sex with men"}},"i":0}}]}'><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt122" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt121" id="mwASc">See also: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blood_donation_restrictions_on_men_who_have_sex_with_men" title="Blood donation restrictions on men who have sex with men">Blood donation restrictions on men who have sex with men</a></div>
<p id="mwASg">Gay and bisexual men were allowed to donate blood following a 12-month deferral period.<sup about="#mwt125" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-36" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-36"}}'><a href="#cite_note-36" id="mwASk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwASo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASs">[</span>36<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASw">]</span></span></a></sup> In 2020, this deferral period was scrapped, with individualised risk assessment introduced.<sup about="#mwt128" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-37" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-37"}}'><a href="#cite_note-37" id="mwAS0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAS4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAS8">[</span>37<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="7" id="mwATE" aria-labelledby="Freedom_of_speech_and_expression"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Freedom_of_speech_and_expression">Freedom of speech and expression</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Hungary&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Freedom of speech and expression"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt129" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwATI" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Main","href":"./Template:Main"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Freedom of speech in Hungary"}},"i":0}}]}'><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt130" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt129" id="mwATM">Main article: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_speech_in_Hungary" title="Freedom of speech in Hungary" class="mw-redirect">Freedom of speech in Hungary</a></div>

<p id="mwATQ">In 2012, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jobbik" title="Jobbik" id="mwATU">Jobbik</a> MP <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ádám_Mirkóczki" title="Ádám Mirkóczki" id="mwATY">Ádám Mirkóczki</a> introduced a constitutional amendment to the Parliament seeking to ban "the promotion of sexual deviations". The amendment would punish the "promotion of homosexuality or other disorders of sexual behaviour" with up to eight years in prison. LMBT Federation, a Hungarian LGBT advocacy group, protested against the amendment and called on Parliament to reject it. The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Coalition_(Hungary)" title="Democratic Coalition (Hungary)" id="mwATc">Democratic Coalition</a> also voiced their opposition and called it "mean and shameful".<sup about="#mwt133" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-38" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-38"}}'><a href="#cite_note-38" id="mwATg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwATk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATo">[</span>38<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATs">]</span></span></a></sup> The amendment ultimately failed to pass.</p>

<p id="mwATw">In November 2016, the small Hungarian town of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ásotthalom" title="Ásotthalom" id="mwAT0">Ásotthalom</a> passed a law banning "gay propaganda", Muslim call to prayer and Muslim clothing. Mayor <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/László_Toroczkai" title="László Toroczkai" id="mwAT4">László Toroczkai</a> (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Our_Homeland_Movement" title="Our Homeland Movement" id="mwAT8">Our Homeland</a>) called on Christians locals to support a "holy war on Muslims and multiculturalism".<sup about="#mwt136" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-39" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-39"}}'><a href="#cite_note-39" id="mwAUA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAUE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUI">[</span>39<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUM">]</span></span></a></sup> In April 2017, after a lawsuit challenging the ban was filed, the Constitutional Court struck down the ban, ruling that it violated human rights law as it aimed to "limit directly the freedom of speech, conscience and religion".<sup about="#mwt139" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-40" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-40"}}'><a href="#cite_note-40" id="mwAUQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAUU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUY">[</span>40<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUc">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAUg">In June 2018, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarian_State_Opera_House" title="Hungarian State Opera House" id="mwAUk">Hungarian State Opera House</a> cancelled 15 <i id="mwAUo"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billy_Elliot_The_Musical" title="Billy Elliot The Musical" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAUs">Billy Elliot</a></i> performances, after pro-government newspaper <i id="mwAUw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magyar_Idők" title="Magyar Idők" id="mwAU0">Magyar Idők</a></i> claimed that the show could <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acquired_homosexuality" title="Acquired homosexuality" id="mwAU4">turn children gay</a>.<sup about="#mwt142" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-41" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-41"}}'><a href="#cite_note-41" id="mwAU8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAVA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVE">[</span>41<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVI">]</span></span></a></sup> However other 29 Billy Elliot performances would be held as planned.<sup about="#mwt145" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-42" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-42"}}'><a href="#cite_note-42" id="mwAVM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAVQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVU">[</span>42<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVY">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt148" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-43" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-43"}}'><a href="#cite_note-43" id="mwAVc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAVg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVk">[</span>43<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVo">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAVs">In November 2020, the town of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagykáta" title="Nagykáta" id="mwAVw">Nagykáta</a> adopted a resolution <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Censorship_of_LGBT_issues" title="Censorship of LGBT issues" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAV0">banning</a> the dissemination and promotion of so-called "<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_propaganda" title="LGBT propaganda" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAV4">LGBT propaganda</a>".<sup about="#mwt151" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-44" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-44"}}'><a href="#cite_note-44" id="mwAV8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAWA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWE">[</span>44<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWI">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<section data-mw-section-id="8" id="mwAWM" aria-labelledby="2021_child_protection_law"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="2021_child_protection_law">2021 child protection law</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Hungary&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: 2021 child protection law"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt152" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAWQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Main","href":"./Template:Main"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Hungarian anti-LGBTQ law"}},"i":0}}]}'><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt153" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt152" id="mwAWU">Main article: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarian_anti-LGBTQ_law" title="Hungarian anti-LGBTQ law">Hungarian anti-LGBTQ law</a></div>

<p id="mwAWY">In June 2021, an anti-<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedophilia" title="Pedophilia" id="mwAWc">pedophilia</a> bill was introduced in the National Assembly, which was originally intended to increase fines for <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_sexual_abuse" title="Child sexual abuse" id="mwAWg">child sexual abuse</a> and the creation, possession, or distribution of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_sexual_abuse_material" title="Child sexual abuse material" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAWk">child sexual abuse material</a> (CSAM). However, shortly before adopting the bill, an amendment was submitted which also criminalized the dissemination of content promoting "divergence from self-identity corresponding to sex at birth, sex change, or homosexuality" to persons under the age of 18.<sup about="#mwt156" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-45" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-45"}}'><a href="#cite_note-45" id="mwAWo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAWs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWw">[</span>45<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAW0">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt159" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-ABC_2021-06-13_46-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"ABC 2021-06-13"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-ABC_2021-06-13-46"}}'><a href="#cite_note-ABC_2021-06-13-46" id="mwAW4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAW8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXA">[</span>46<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXE">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt162" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-47" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-47"}}'><a href="#cite_note-47" id="mwAXI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAXM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXQ">[</span>47<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXU">]</span></span></a></sup> On 15 June, the bill was passed by a vote of 157–1.<sup about="#mwt165" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-AP_NEWS_2021_48-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"AP NEWS 2021"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-AP_NEWS_2021-48"}}'><a href="#cite_note-AP_NEWS_2021-48" id="mwAXY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAXc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXg">[</span>48<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXk">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt168" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Bollinger_2021_49-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Bollinger 2021"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Bollinger_2021-49"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Bollinger_2021-49" id="mwAXo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAXs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAXw">[</span>49<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAX0">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt171" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-The_Christian_Science_Monitor_2021_50-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"The Christian Science Monitor 2021"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-The_Christian_Science_Monitor_2021-50"}}'><a href="#cite_note-The_Christian_Science_Monitor_2021-50" id="mwAX4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAX8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYA">[</span>50<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYE">]</span></span></a></sup> President János Áder signed the bill into law on 23 June 2021, and it came into effect on 1 July.<sup about="#mwt174" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-The_President's_Office_2021_51-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"The President&apos;s Office 2021"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-The_President&apos;s_Office_2021-51"}}'><a href="#cite_note-The_President's_Office_2021-51" id="mwAYI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAYM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYQ">[</span>51<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYU">]</span></span></a></sup> A Hungarian government spokesperson claimed the ban is intended to prevent children from accessing content that "[they] can misunderstand and which may have a detrimental effect on their development".<sup about="#mwt177" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Guardian_2021-06-15_52-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Guardian 2021-06-15"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Guardian_2021-06-15-52"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Guardian_2021-06-15-52" id="mwAYY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAYc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYg">[</span>52<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYk">]</span></span></a></sup></p> 

<p id="mwAYo">Per the law, a decree was issued to require stores offering children's books containing homosexuality or gender transition content to seal them in a "closed wrapping" and place them away from other books, and prohibiting their sale at any location within 200 metres of a church or school.<sup about="#mwt180" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-53" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-53"}}'><a href="#cite_note-53" id="mwAYs"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAYw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAY0">[</span>53<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAY4">]</span></span></a></sup> The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Media_and_Infocommunications_Authority" title="National Media and Infocommunications Authority" id="mwAY8">National Media and Infocommunications Authority</a> similarly issued guidance stating that films and television programmes where homosexuality or gender transitions are a "defining feature" would not be considered suitable for viewers under the age of 18.<sup about="#mwt183" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-54" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-54"}}'><a href="#cite_note-54" id="mwAZA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAZE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZI">[</span>54<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZM">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAZQ">The law faced criticism from human rights groups, which argued that the bill infringed upon freedom of expression, would prevent minors from being able to access resources and support, and would harm their mental health. David Vig of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amnesty_International" title="Amnesty International" id="mwAZU">Amnesty International</a> compared the bill to Russia's similar <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_anti-LGBTQ_law" title="Russian anti-LGBTQ law" id="mwAZY">LGBT "propaganda" law</a>, arguing that it "[would] further stigmatize LGBTI people, exposing them to greater discrimination in what is already a hostile environment."<sup about="#mwt186" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Reuters_2021-06-11_55-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Reuters 2021-06-11"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Reuters_2021-06-11-55"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Reuters_2021-06-11-55" id="mwAZc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAZg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZk">[</span>55<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZo">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt187" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-ABC_2021-06-13_46-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"ABC 2021-06-13"}}'><a href="#cite_note-ABC_2021-06-13-46" id="mwAZs"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAZw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZ0">[</span>46<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZ4">]</span></span></a></sup> Seventeen EU member states denounced the bill as a breach of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charter_of_Fundamental_Rights_of_the_European_Union" title="Charter of Fundamental Rights of the European Union" id="mwAZ8">Charter of Fundamental Rights of the European Union</a> (CFR).<sup about="#mwt190" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-56" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-56"}}'><a href="#cite_note-56" id="mwAaA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAaE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAaI">[</span>56<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAaM">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAaQ">In July 2021, it was reported that the European Commission would file two lawsuits challenging the law, with one suit alleging that it violated freedom of expression, and was inconsistent with the EU's <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Audiovisual_Media_Services_Directive_2010" title="Audiovisual Media Services Directive 2010" id="mwAaU">Audiovisual Media Services Directive</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_Commerce_Directive_2000" title="Electronic Commerce Directive 2000" id="mwAaY">Electronic Commerce Directive</a>. The second challenged the government's efforts to censure a children's book published by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labrisz_Lesbian_Association" title="Labrisz Lesbian Association" id="mwAac">Labrisz</a>, in violation of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unfair_Commercial_Practices_Directive_2005" title="Unfair Commercial Practices Directive 2005" id="mwAag">Unfair Commercial Practices Directive</a>.<sup about="#mwt196" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-57" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-57"}}'><a href="#cite_note-57" id="mwAak"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAao"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAas">[</span>57<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAaw">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt199" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-58" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-58"}}'><a href="#cite_note-58" id="mwAa0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAa4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAa8">[</span>58<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbA">]</span></span></a></sup> The Commission initiated infringement proceedings against Hungary on 15 July, and subsequently issued a reasoned opinion on 2 December 2021, highlighting the country’s failure to meet its obligations under various EU directives and articles concerning audiovisual media services, electronic commerce, internal market services, data protection, and fundamental rights.With Hungary's response deemed unsatisfactory, on July 15, 2022, the Commission referred Hungary to the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Court_of_Justice" title="European Court of Justice" id="mwAbE">European Court of Justice</a>. As of April 2023, 15 member states had joined the suit as third-parties, making it the largest civil rights case in EU history.<b id="mwAbI"><sup about="#mwt202" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-59" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-59"}}'><a href="#cite_note-59" id="mwAbM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAbQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbU">[</span>59<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbY">]</span></span></a></sup></b><sup about="#mwt205" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-60" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-60"}}'><a href="#cite_note-60" id="mwAbc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAbg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbk">[</span>60<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbo">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt208" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-61" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-61"}}'><a href="#cite_note-61" id="mwAbs"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAbw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAb0">[</span>61<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAb4">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAb8">The government would hold a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Hungarian_LGBTQ_in_education_referendum" title="2022 Hungarian LGBTQ in education referendum" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAcA">referendum on LGBTQ topics in children's education</a> on 3 April 2022 alongside the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Hungarian_parliamentary_election" title="2022 Hungarian parliamentary election" id="mwAcE">2022 elections</a>.<sup about="#mwt211" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-62" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-62"}}'><a href="#cite_note-62" id="mwAcI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAcM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcQ">[</span>62<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcU">]</span></span></a></sup> The referendum was countered by Amnesty International Hungary, Háttér Society, and 12 other civil rights organizations mobilizing over 1.7 million people; the referendum did not reach the threshold of 50% valid votes in order to pass.<sup about="#mwt214" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-BT_63-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"BT"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-BT-63"}}'><a href="#cite_note-BT-63" id="mwAcY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAcc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcg">[</span>63<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAck">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt217" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-64" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-64"}}'><a href="#cite_note-64" id="mwAco"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAcs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcw">[</span>64<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAc0">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt220" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-65" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-65"}}'><a href="#cite_note-65" id="mwAc4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAc8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAdA">[</span>65<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAdE">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt223" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-66" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-66"}}'><a href="#cite_note-66" id="mwAdI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAdM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAdQ">[</span>66<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAdU">]</span></span></a></sup></p>  

<p id="mwAdY">The rapid spread of similar anti-LGBTQ laws, particularly in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_state_of_the_European_Union" title="Member state of the European Union" id="mwAdc">EU member states</a> (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_rights_in_Bulgaria#2024_anti-LGBT_legislation" title="LGBTQ rights in Bulgaria" id="mwAdg">Bulgaria</a>, August 2024) or <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potential_enlargement_of_the_European_Union" title="Potential enlargement of the European Union" id="mwAdk">candidates</a> (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgian_anti-LGBT_law" title="Georgian anti-LGBT law" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAdo">Georgia</a>, October 2024), reinforced the argument that the EU’s failure to decisively penalize Hungary for its 2021 law demonstrated weakness, created a sense of impunity, and provided a confidence boost to other governments proposing or adopting similar anti-LGBTQ laws. Human rights organizations like <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_Out_(organisation)" title="All Out (organisation)" id="mwAds">All Out</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ILGA-Europe" title="ILGA-Europe" id="mwAdw">ILGA-Europe</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Rights_Watch" title="Human Rights Watch" id="mwAd0">Human Rights Watch</a> have argued that the EU's reaction was either too slow, not forceful enough, or failed to use all available mechanisms promptly.<sup about="#mwt226" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-67" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-67"}}'><a href="#cite_note-67" id="mwAd4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAd8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAeA">[</span>67<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAeE">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt229" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-68" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-68"}}'><a href="#cite_note-68" id="mwAeI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAeM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAeQ">[</span>68<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAeU">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt232" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-69" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-69"}}'><a href="#cite_note-69" id="mwAeY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAec"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAeg">[</span>69<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAek">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAeo">On 22 April 2026, the European Court of Justice <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Commission_v_Hungary" title="European Commission v Hungary" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAes">ruled</a> that the law violated Article 1 of the EU Charter of Fundamental Rights and Article 2 on the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_on_European_Union" title="Treaty on European Union" id="mwAew">Treaty on European Union</a>, and ordered the Hungarian government to amend or repeal the legislation to comply with European law.<sup about="#mwt235" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:3_70-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":3"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:3-70"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:3-70" id="mwAe0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAe4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAe8">[</span>70<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfA">]</span></span></a></sup> The court found that the law violated the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fundamental_rights" title="Fundamental rights" id="mwAfE">fundamental rights</a> of LGBTI residents by discriminating and scapegoating against them, and that the law was "contrary to the very identity of the Union as a common legal order in a society in which pluralism prevails".<sup about="#mwt238" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:1_71-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":1"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:1-71"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:1-71" id="mwAfI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAfM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfQ">[</span>71<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfU">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt241" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:2_72-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":2"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:2-72"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:2-72" id="mwAfY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAfc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfg">[</span>72<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfk">]</span></span></a></sup> It marked the first time that either article had been invoked in a ruling by the European Court of Justice.<sup about="#mwt242" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:1_71-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":1"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:1-71" id="mwAfo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAfs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfw">[</span>71<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAf0">]</span></span></a></sup></p> 

<p id="mwAf4">In September 2026, Prime Minister <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Péter_Magyar" title="Péter Magyar" id="mwAf8">Péter Magyar</a> announced that the statute would be amended to comply with the European Court's ruling, primarily by removing provisions related to the promotion of homosexuality and gender identity, leaving prohibitions on distributing material containing violent or sexual content that would impairs a child's "physical, intellectual, emotional or moral development" to minors.<sup about="#mwt245" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:5_73-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":5"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:5-73"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:5-73" id="mwAgA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAgE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgI">[</span>73<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgM">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt248" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:6_74-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":6"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:6-74"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:6-74" id="mwAgQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAgU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgY">[</span>74<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgc">]</span></span></a></sup></p>
