</section><section data-mw-section-id="9" id="mwAgg" aria-labelledby="2025_Pride_ban"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="2025_Pride_ban">2025 Pride ban</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Hungary&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: 2025 Pride ban"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt249" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAgk" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Main","href":"./Template:Main"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"2025 Hungarian Pride ban"}},"i":0}}]}'><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt250" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt249" id="mwAgo">Main article: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Hungarian_Pride_ban" title="2025 Hungarian Pride ban" class="mw-redirect">2025 Hungarian Pride ban</a></div>

<p id="mwAgs">In March 2025, the Hungarian government passed legislation making it illegal to organize or participate in a public assembly that promotes homosexuality or gender changes to minors in violation of the child protection law. The legislation was intended as a legal basis to prohibit <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pride_parade" title="Pride parade" id="mwAgw">pride parades</a> and similar events, and permitted authorities to use facial recognition software to identify those attending such events.<sup about="#mwt253" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-75" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-75"}}'><a href="#cite_note-75" id="mwAg0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAg4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAg8">[</span>75<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt256" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-76" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-76"}}'><a href="#cite_note-76" id="mwAhE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAhI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhM">[</span>76<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhQ">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt259" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-telex-english_77-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"telex-english"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-telex-english-77"}}'><a href="#cite_note-telex-english-77" id="mwAhU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAhY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhc">[</span>77<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhg">]</span></span></a></sup> The law sparked a protest outside Parliament which was attended by several thousand. The protesters chanted anti-government slogans and staged a blockade of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margaret_Bridge" title="Margaret Bridge" id="mwAhk">Margaret Bridge</a> over the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danube" title="Danube" id="mwAho">Danube</a>, blocking traffic and disregarding police instructions to leave the area.<sup about="#mwt262" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-78" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-78"}}'><a href="#cite_note-78" id="mwAhs"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAhw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAh0">[</span>78<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAh4">]</span></span></a></sup></p>  

<p id="mwAh8">On 3 June 2025, the Budapest police denied permits for the 30th edition of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budapest_Pride" title="Budapest Pride" id="mwAiA">Budapest Pride</a> to be held on 28 June, citing the law. The police argued that "it cannot be ruled out, or is even inevitable, that a person under the age of 18 will be able to engage in legally prohibited conduct" should they attend the march. Organizers responded by calling the decision "textbook example of tyranny" and vowed that the event would go on as planned. Mayor of Budapest <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gergely_Karácsony" title="Gergely Karácsony" id="mwAiE">Gergely Karácsony</a> attempted to reclassify the parade as a "municipal celebration" of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungary–Soviet_Union_relations#End_of_Soviet_military_presence" title="Hungary–Soviet Union relations" id="mwAiI">1991 withdrawal of the Soviet Union from Hungary</a> which, therefore, did not require a permit, but Budapest police asserted that Budapest Pride remained "banned".<sup about="#mwt265" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-79" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-79"}}'><a href="#cite_note-79" id="mwAiM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAiQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAiU">[</span>79<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAiY">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt268" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-80" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-80"}}'><a href="#cite_note-80" id="mwAic"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAig"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAik">[</span>80<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAio">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt271" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-81" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-81"}}'><a href="#cite_note-81" id="mwAis"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAiw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAi0">[</span>81<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAi4">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt274" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:0_82-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":0"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:0-82"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:0-82" id="mwAi8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAjA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAjE">[</span>82<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAjI">]</span></span></a></sup> The parade saw between 100,000 and 200,000 participants and no arrests, in comparison to 35,000 in 2024, placing it among the country's largest anti-governmental protests; attendees protested both in support of LGBT rights, and in opposition towards the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_premiership_of_Viktor_Orbán" title="Second premiership of Viktor Orbán" id="mwAjM">Orbán government</a>.<sup about="#mwt275" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:0_82-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":0"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:0-82" id="mwAjQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAjU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAjY">[</span>82<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAjc">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt278" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-83" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-83"}}'><a href="#cite_note-83" id="mwAjg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAjk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAjo">[</span>83<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAjs">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt281" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-84" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-84"}}'><a href="#cite_note-84" id="mwAjw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAj0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAj4">[</span>84<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAj8">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt284" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-85" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-85"}}'><a href="#cite_note-85" id="mwAkA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAkE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAkI">[</span>85<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAkM">]</span></span></a></sup> In January 2026, Karácsony was criminally charged with organising a public assembly without police authorisation.<sup about="#mwt287" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-86" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-86"}}'><a href="#cite_note-86" id="mwAkQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAkU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAkY">[</span>86<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAkc">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt290" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-87" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-87"}}'><a href="#cite_note-87" id="mwAkg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAkk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAko">[</span>87<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAks">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAkw">In May 2026, it was reported that Budapest authorities had granted permits for Budapest Pride, under the basis that there were no longer legal grounds to prohibit the event due to the European Court of Justice's April 2026 ruling.<sup about="#mwt293" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-88" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-88"}}'><a href="#cite_note-88" id="mwAk0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAk4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAk8">[</span>88<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAlA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt296" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-89" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-89"}}'><a href="#cite_note-89" id="mwAlE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAlI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAlM">[</span>89<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAlQ">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt299" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-90" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-90"}}'><a href="#cite_note-90" id="mwAlU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAlY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAlc">[</span>90<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAlg">]</span></span></a></sup> Criminal charges against Karácsony were also dropped, with prosecutors also citing the EU ruling.<sup about="#mwt302" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-91" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-91"}}'><a href="#cite_note-91" id="mwAlk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAlo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAls">[</span>91<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAlw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="10" id="mwAl0" aria-labelledby="Living_conditions"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Living_conditions">Living conditions</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Hungary&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Living conditions"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwAl4"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Gaypride_Budapest_assoc.JPG" class="mw-file-description" id="mwAl8"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Gaypride_Budapest_assoc.JPG" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Gaypride_Budapest_assoc.JPG/250px-Gaypride_Budapest_assoc.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Gaypride_Budapest_assoc.JPG/500px-Gaypride_Budapest_assoc.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="480" data-file-type="bitmap" height="188" width="250" class="mw-file-element" id="mwAmA"/></a><figcaption id="mwAmE">2008 <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budapest" title="Budapest" id="mwAmI">Budapest</a> Gay Dignity Procession (<i id="mwAmM">Meleg Méltóság Menet</i>)</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwAmQ"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Budapest_Pride_2017.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwAmU"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Budapest_Pride_2017.jpg" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0c/Budapest_Pride_2017.jpg/250px-Budapest_Pride_2017.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0c/Budapest_Pride_2017.jpg/500px-Budapest_Pride_2017.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" data-file-width="4288" data-file-height="2848" data-file-type="bitmap" height="166" width="250" class="mw-file-element" id="mwAmY"/></a><figcaption id="mwAmc">2017 <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budapest_Pride" title="Budapest Pride" id="mwAmg">Budapest Pride</a> Festival</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwAmk">Hungary was the host country of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mr_Gay_Europe" title="Mr Gay Europe" id="mwAmo">Mr Gay Europe</a> 2007 contest and the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EuroGames" title="EuroGames" id="mwAms">EuroGames</a> in 2012.</p>

<p id="mwAmw">Budapest Pride was the first such event in the former <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Bloc" title="Eastern Bloc" id="mwAm0">Eastern Bloc</a>, and draws a steady, but a moderate number of LGBTQ people and their supporters. The LGBTQ festival lasts a week every summer with a film festival, a pride march and parties across the city. The festival was opened in the past by notable public figures including <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gábor_Demszky" title="Gábor Demszky" id="mwAm4">Gábor Demszky</a>, then <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_of_Budapest" title="Mayor of Budapest" id="mwAm8">Mayor of Budapest</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kinga_Göncz" title="Kinga Göncz" id="mwAnA">Kinga Göncz</a>, then <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_Foreign_Affairs_(Hungary)" title="Minister of Foreign Affairs (Hungary)" id="mwAnE">Minister of Foreign Affairs</a>. The Hungarian far-right party <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Our_Homeland_Movement" title="Our Homeland Movement" id="mwAnI">Our Homeland Movement</a> has called for a ban on LGBTQ pride marches.<sup about="#mwt305" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-92" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-92"}}'><a href="#cite_note-92" id="mwAnM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAnQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAnU">[</span>92<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAnY">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt308" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-93" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-93"}}'><a href="#cite_note-93" id="mwAnc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAng"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAnk">[</span>93<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAno">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAns">In correlation with the prime ministership of Viktor Orbán, LGBTQ rights in Hungary stalled. In March 2016, the Hungarian Government blocked a proposed European Union agreement to combat discrimination against LGBTQ people.<sup about="#mwt311" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-94" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-94"}}'><a href="#cite_note-94" id="mwAnw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAn0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAn4">[</span>94<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAn8">]</span></span></a></sup> In May 2017, Prime Minister Orbán welcomed the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Congress_of_Families" title="World Congress of Families" id="mwAoA">World Congress of Families</a>, a designated <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hate_group" title="Hate group" id="mwAoE">hate group</a> by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Poverty_Law_Center" title="Southern Poverty Law Center" id="mwAoI">Southern Poverty Law Center</a>, at the National Parliament.<sup about="#mwt314" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-95" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-95"}}'><a href="#cite_note-95" id="mwAoM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAoQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAoU">[</span>95<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAoY">]</span></span></a></sup> In 2018, Hungary and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poland" title="Poland" id="mwAoc">Poland</a> blocked a joint statement by EU employment and social affairs ministers intended to promote gender equity in the digital era because of objections to a reference to LGBTQ people. However, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austria" title="Austria" id="mwAog">Austria</a>—then president of the Council of the European Union—adopted the text regardless, though with modifications. While the reference to LGBTQ people was retained, the text was classified as "presidential conclusions" which do not carry the legal weight of formal Council conclusions.<sup about="#mwt317" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-96" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-96"}}'><a href="#cite_note-96" id="mwAok"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAoo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAos">[</span>96<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAow">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt320" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-97" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-97"}}'><a href="#cite_note-97" id="mwAo0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAo4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAo8">[</span>97<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwApA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwApE">In recent years, more and more politicians have resorted to use openly <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homophobic_rhetoric" title="Homophobic rhetoric" class="mw-redirect" id="mwApI">homophobic rhetoric</a>.<sup about="#mwt323" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-98" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-98"}}'><a href="#cite_note-98" id="mwApM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwApQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwApU">[</span>98<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwApY">]</span></span></a></sup> In 2014, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jobbik" title="Jobbik" id="mwApc">Jobbik</a> displayed a sign reading "The Parliament Does Not Want Any Deviants" during Budapest Pride, and verbally abused attendees and defaced posters in support of LGBTQ rights. In November 2016, it protested the painting of a fence with rainbow colours in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pomáz" title="Pomáz" id="mwApg">Pomáz</a>, even though the colouring had no connections to LGBTQ rights.<sup about="#mwt326" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-99" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-99"}}'><a href="#cite_note-99" id="mwApk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwApo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAps">[</span>99<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwApw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAp0">The 2017 Budapest Pride parade attracted thousands of people, and received the support of many embassies, including from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia" title="Australia" id="mwAp4">Australia</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canada" title="Canada" id="mwAp8">Canada</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France" title="France" id="mwAqA">France</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germany" title="Germany" id="mwAqE">Germany</a>, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Kingdom" title="United Kingdom" id="mwAqI">United Kingdom</a> and the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States" title="United States" id="mwAqM">United States</a>, as well as neighbouring <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slovakia" title="Slovakia" id="mwAqQ">Slovakia</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slovenia" title="Slovenia" id="mwAqU">Slovenia</a>, among others.<sup about="#mwt329" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-100" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-100"}}'><a href="#cite_note-100" id="mwAqY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAqc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAqg">[</span>100<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAqk">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAqo">In January 2018, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Court_of_Justice" title="European Court of Justice" id="mwAqs">European Court of Justice</a> ruled that asylum seekers may not be subjected by authorities to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fruit_machine_(homosexuality_test)" title="Fruit machine (homosexuality test)" id="mwAqw">psychological tests in order to determine their sexual orientation</a>.<sup about="#mwt332" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-101" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-101"}}'><a href="#cite_note-101" id="mwAq0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAq4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAq8">[</span>101<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwArE">On 12 April 2026, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tisza_Party" title="Tisza Party" id="mwArI">Tisza Party</a> defeated Fidesz in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Hungarian_parliamentary_election" title="2026 Hungarian parliamentary election" id="mwArM">parliamentary election</a>, ending Viktor Orbán's 16-year regime as prime minister of Hungary.<sup about="#mwt335" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Politico_2026_102-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Politico 2026"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Politico_2026-102"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Politico_2026-102" id="mwArQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwArU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArY">[</span>102<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArc">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt338" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-103" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-103"}}'><a href="#cite_note-103" id="mwArg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwArk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAro">[</span>103<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArs">]</span></span></a></sup> The party restrained from explicitly campaigning on LGBTQ rights, but party leader and prime minister-designate <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Péter_Magyar" title="Péter Magyar" id="mwArw">Péter Magyar</a> did express support for <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_assembly" title="Freedom of assembly" id="mwAr0">freedom of assembly</a> in opposition to the attempts to ban Pride parades, and stated during a victory speech that Hungary should be a country "where no one is stigmatised for thinking differently than the majority, or loving someone differently than the majority".<sup about="#mwt341" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:4_104-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":4"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:4-104"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:4-104" id="mwAr4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAr8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAsA">[</span>104<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAsE">]</span></span></a></sup></p> 

<p id="mwAsI">While these remarks were seen as indicating that Magyar's government may take a more supportive stance on LGBTQ rights in comparison to Orbán,<sup about="#mwt344" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-105" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-105"}}'><a href="#cite_note-105" id="mwAsM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAsQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAsU">[</span>105<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAsY">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt347" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-106" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-106"}}'><a href="#cite_note-106" id="mwAsc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAsg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAsk">[</span>106<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAso">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt350" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-107" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-107"}}'><a href="#cite_note-107" id="mwAss"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAsw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAs0">[</span>107<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAs4">]</span></span></a></sup> some LGBT rights activists expressed concerns over the party's lack of specific commitments or timeline.<sup about="#mwt351" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:4_104-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":4"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:4-104" id="mwAs8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAtA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAtE">[</span>104<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAtI">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt354" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-108" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-108"}}'><a href="#cite_note-108" id="mwAtM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAtQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAtU">[</span>108<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAtY">]</span></span></a></sup> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Rights_Watch" title="Human Rights Watch" id="mwAtc">Human Rights Watch</a> called upon the new government to ensure the 2021 child protection law was repealed or amended in compliance with the European Court of Justice's ruling.<sup about="#mwt355" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:3_70-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":3"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:3-70" id="mwAtg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAtk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAto">[</span>70<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAts">]</span></span></a></sup> In September 2026, Magyar announced his intent to begin repealing Orbán-era policies, including the ban on adoption by same-sex couples, and planned amendments to the child protection law to remove provisions prohibiting the promotion of homosexuality and gender identity.<sup about="#mwt356" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:5_73-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":5"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:5-73" id="mwAtw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAt0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAt4">[</span>73<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAt8">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt357" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:6_74-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":6"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:6-74" id="mwAuA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAuE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAuI">[</span>74<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAuM">]</span></span></a></sup></p> 

</section><section data-mw-section-id="11" id="mwAuQ" aria-labelledby="Public_opinion"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Public_opinion">Public opinion</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Hungary&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Public opinion"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

class="pie-thumb thumb tright" about="#mwt358" id="mwAuY"><div class="thumbinner"><div class="smooth-pie-container" style="">
<div class="smooth-pie-legend-container"><div class="smooth-pie-caption">Public opinion on same-sex marriage (2026 Medián poll)</div>

<div class="smooth-pie-border"></div></div>
</div></div></div>
<p id="mwAuc">According to a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurobarometer" title="Eurobarometer" id="mwAug">Eurobarometer</a> survey published in December 2006, only 18% of Hungarians surveyed supported same-sex marriage, and only 13% recognized a same-sex couple's right to adopt, compared to the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union" id="mwAuk">EU</a>-wide average of 44% and 33%, respectively.<sup about="#mwt362" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-EU_109-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"EU"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-EU-109"}}'><a href="#cite_note-EU-109" id="mwAuo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAus"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAuw">[</span>109<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAu0">]</span></span></a></sup> However, a poll conducted a year after, in 2007, indicated that 30% of the Hungarian public supported same-sex marriage.<sup about="#mwt365" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Medián_110-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Medián"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Medián-110"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Medián-110" id="mwAu4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAu8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAvA">[</span>110<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAvE">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAvI">The poll taken in 2015 found 39% of Hungarians supported same-sex marriage. A poll by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center" title="Pew Research Center" id="mwAvM">Pew Research Center</a>, published in May 2017, found that 27% of Hungarians were in favor of same-sex marriage, while 64% opposed it. Support was higher among non-religious people (34%) and 18–34 year olds (39%), in contrast to Catholics (25%) and people aged 35 and over (23%).<sup about="#mwt368" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Pew_Survey_2017_111-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Pew Survey 2017"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Pew_Survey_2017-111"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Pew_Survey_2017-111" id="mwAvQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAvU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAvY">[</span>111<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAvc">]</span></span></a></sup> In May 2015, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PlanetRomeo" title="PlanetRomeo" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAvg">PlanetRomeo</a>, an LGBTQ social network, published its first Gay Happiness Index (GHI). <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gay_men" title="Gay men" id="mwAvk">Gay men</a> from over 120 countries were asked about how they feel about society's view on homosexuality, how they experience the way they are treated by other people and how satisfied are they with their lives. Hungary was ranked 49th with a GHI score of 47.<sup about="#mwt370" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-112" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-112"}}'><a href="#cite_note-112" id="mwAvo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAvs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAvw">[</span>112<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAv0">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAv4">According to a 2017 poll carried out by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Lesbian,_Gay,_Bisexual,_Trans_and_Intersex_Association" title="International Lesbian, Gay, Bisexual, Trans and Intersex Association" id="mwAv8">ILGA</a>, 64% of Hungarians agreed that gay, lesbian and bisexual people should enjoy the same rights as straight people, while 15% disagreed. Additionally, 69% agreed that they should be protected from workplace discrimination. 13% of Hungarians, however, said that people who are in same-sex relationships should be charged as criminals, while 64% disagreed. As for transgender people, 60% agreed that they should have the same rights, 64% believed they should be protected from employment discrimination and a plurality of 48% believed they should be allowed to change their legal gender.<sup about="#mwt372" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-113" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-113"}}'><a href="#cite_note-113" id="mwAwA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAwE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAwI">[</span>113<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAwM">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAwQ">A poll by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center" title="Pew Research Center" id="mwAwU">Pew Research Center</a>, published in 2023, found that 31% of Hungarians were in favor of same-sex marriage, while 63% opposed it.<sup about="#mwt375" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-auto_114-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"auto"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-auto-114"}}'><a href="#cite_note-auto-114" id="mwAwY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAwc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAwg">[</span>114<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAwk">]</span></span></a></sup> A <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GLOBSEC" title="GLOBSEC" id="mwAwo">GLOBSEC</a> survey conducted in March 2023 showed that 56% of Hungarians supported same-sex marriage, while 37% were opposed.<sup about="#mwt378" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-globsec_115-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"globsec"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-globsec-115"}}'><a href="#cite_note-globsec-115" id="mwAws"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAww"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAw0">[</span>115<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAw4">]</span></span></a></sup> The 2023 Eurobarometer found that 42% of Hungarians thought same-sex marriage should be allowed throughout Europe, and 49% agreed that "there is nothing wrong in a sexual relationship between two persons of the same sex".<sup about="#mwt381" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-116" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-116"}}'><a href="#cite_note-116" id="mwAw8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAxA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAxE">[</span>116<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAxI">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAxM">A 2026 Medián poll following <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Hungarian_parliamentary_election" title="2026 Hungarian parliamentary election" id="mwAxQ">the elections</a> showed that 68% of Hungarians supported same-sex marriage and adoption rights, 71% supported allowing <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_insemination" title="Artificial insemination" id="mwAxU">artificial insemination</a> for lesbians, and 79% agreed that it is the state's duty to act against LGBT discrimination. The poll also showed that 57% of Hungarians supported the reversal of anti-LGBT policies, 23% supported keeping them as-is, and 10% supported further restrictions.<sup about="#mwt384" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-117" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-117"}}'><a href="#cite_note-117" id="mwAxY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAxc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAxg">[</span>117<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAxk">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt387" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-118" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-118"}}'><a href="#cite_note-118" id="mwAxo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAxs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAxw">[</span>118<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAx0">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="12" id="mwAx4" aria-labelledby="Summary_table"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Summary_table">Summary table</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=LGBTQ_rights_in_Hungary&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Summary table"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

<td id="mwA7w"><span typeof="mw:File" id="mwA70" data-mw='{"caption":"Yes"}'><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Yes_check.svg" class="mw-file-description" title="Yes" id="mwA74"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Yes_check.svg" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/f/fb/Yes_check.svg/20px-Yes_check.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/f/fb/Yes_check.svg/40px-Yes_check.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" alt="Yes" data-file-width="600" data-file-height="600" data-file-type="drawing" height="15" width="15" class="mw-file-element" id="mwA78"/></a></span> (Since 2020)</td></tr>
